# Rust seam census for the stage-3 fleet (2026-08-05)

Agent-run census at the W1/W2/W3 merge tip, before W4. This document is the
factual basis for the Rust extraction. The verdict summary: Rust needs two more
prerequisites before its extraction can hit the shim bar -- a core
PreparedSyntaxTree contract (W5) and an impl-RustAnalyzer-to-free-function
rewrite (R1) -- and one recorded deviation: moka enters the rust crate with the
RUST_TREES parse memo, because a global parse cache is language-crate state.

{"parentUuid":"7f7f5682-56a4-438b-b36a-eaa38ba5609e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d22550a0-292d-4e69-b601-f2ff8a7e9aed","agentId":"a296f2bebb99cef04","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015t3YNKVvmz7TwsGvmzXsZk","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Stage 3 pilot: extract Go into brokk-bifrost-go on analysis-owned shims\n2\t\n3\tApproved by Jonathan 2026-08-04 (\"run the pilot and if it works as expected, finish\n4\tstage 3\"). This plan executes the milestone-3 recommendation of\n5\t`.agents/plans/analysis-language-registry-spi.md`: one pilot extraction, analysis-owned\n6\tshims, only the types the pilot consumes lowered, measured before any fleet decision.\n7\tThe factual basis is the seam census taken at `6bcd3cdb` (recorded in the decision log\n8\tbelow and summarized here); LOC figures and file citations come from it. Line numbers\n9\tdrift; search.\n10\t\n11\t## Shape\n12\t\n13\tNew workspace crate `crates/bifrost-go` (package `brokk-bifrost-go`), sitting between\n14\t`brokk-bifrost-core` and `brokk-bifrost-analysis`: it depends only on core (plus\n15\tcrates.io: tree-sitter, tree-sitter-go, rayon, regex, serde, semver), and analysis\n16\tdepends on it. It holds Go *language knowledge* as plain functions, data, and types.\n17\tAnalysis keeps the shim: `GoAnalyzer` (a newtype over `TreeSitterAnalyzer<GoAdapter>`\n18\tthat CANNOT leave), the `GoAdapter` forwarding shell, the SPI block\n19\t(`GoSupport`/edge pass/dead-code bulk), the trait-impl wrappers\n20\t(`GoQueryResolver`/`GoEdgeResolver`/`GoUsageGraphStrategy`), the capability-provider\n21\timpls, `GoMemoCaches`, and the one-line `impl_program_semantics_provider!` invocation.\n22\tEstimated shim ~1,050 LOC, most of it pre-existing forwarding that merely stays.\n23\t\n24\tMoves to `brokk-bifrost-go` (~10.3k LOC): `packages.rs`, `structural.rs` (after the\n25\tadapter_helpers promotion), the import-analysis logic from `imports.rs`,\n26\t`declarations.rs`, `tests.rs` (Go test detection — production code), `hierarchy.rs`,\n27\t`diagnostics.rs`, the 12 pure `GoAdapter` method bodies as free functions +\n28\t`GO_COGNITIVE_CONFIG`, `GO_CLONE_SYNTAX`, and all six `go_graph` files (the\n29\twhole-workspace inverted pass reshaped per milestone P0's split). The\n30\t`resources/treesitter/go/*.scm` queries move with the crate; the store epoch salt path\n31\t(`analyzer/store/epoch.rs`, `\"treesitter/go/\"`) must be updated to the new location and\n32\tthe Go `lang_epoch!` salt BUMPED, since query-file relocation changes what the salt\n33\thashes.\n34\t\n35\tStays in analysis, explicitly, with the reason recorded:\n36\t- `go/semantic.rs` (3,265 LOC): inseparable from the 36.8k-LOC `analyzer/semantic`\n37\t  subsystem; its registration macro textually requires the `TreeSitterAnalyzer` field.\n38\t- `go/artifact.rs` + `go/dependency_discovery.rs` (4,353 LOC): gated on\n39\t  `semantic_model/` (17k LOC) and `process.rs`. Lowering `semantic_model` is a named\n40\t  fleet-phase workstream, not a pilot blocker.\n41\t- `get_definition/go.rs` + `get_type/go.rs` (3,479 LOC): gated on\n42\t  `SemanticModelOverlay`, `ResolutionSession`, `GlobalUsageDefinitionIndex`. Same\n43\t  fleet-phase workstream.\n44\t- `go/cache.rs`: `GoMemoCaches` is shim state (keeps moka out of the go crate and out\n45\t  of core); it may cache go-crate-defined types (e.g. `GoHierarchyIndex`).\n46\t\n47\tGo-crate functions that today take `&dyn IAnalyzer` take `&dyn CodeUnitIndex` (core)\n48\tplus explicit Go side-data instead; every `resolve_analyzer::<GoAnalyzer>` downcast\n49\thappens in the shim.\n50\t\n51\t## Milestones\n52\t\n53\tP0 — fleet-reusable lowerings into `brokk-bifrost-core` (no Go crate yet; analysis\n54\tre-exports at old paths; every downstream crate compiles unchanged):\n55\t1. `analyzer/usages/model.rs` (755 LOC — imports ONLY CodeUnit/ProjectFile/Range/hash;\n56\t   the highest-leverage single move) and `outcome.rs` (82).\n57\t2. `local_inference.rs` (356), `reference_site.rs` (458; core already has\n58\t   tree-sitter), `receiver_analysis.rs` (624), `type_relations.rs` (121),\n59\t   `graph_core::{ImportEdge, ImportEdgeKind}`, `reexport_seeds.rs` (147).\n60\t3. SPLIT `inverted_edges.rs`: data types (`UsageEdges<K>`, `UsageEdgeWeights<K>`,\n61\t   `CallSite`, `UsageNodeKey`, `NodeKey`, `UsageReferenceCounts`) to core; the driver\n62\t   (`EdgeCollector`, `parse_and_collect`, `build_edge_output`, `ClassRangeIndex`)\n63\t   stays in analysis. `LanguageEdgePass` signatures now traffic in core types.\n64\t4. Extract `UsageScanScope` from `traits.rs` (its co-residents `UsageAnalyzer`/\n65\t   `GraphUsageAnalyzer` name IAnalyzer and stay).\n66\t5. This plan executes the milestone-3 recommendation of\n`.agents/plans/analysis-language-registry-spi.md`: one pilot extraction, analysis-owned\nshims, only the types the pilot consumes lowered, measured before any fleet decision.\nThe factual basis is the seam census taken at `6bcd3cdb` (recorded in the decision log\nbelow and summarized here); LOC figures and file citations come from it. Line numbers\ndrift; search.\n\n## Shape\n\nNew workspace crate `crates/bifrost-go` (package `brokk-bifrost-go`), sitting between\n`brokk-bifrost-core` and `brokk-bifrost-analysis`: it depends only on core (plus\ncrates.io: tree-sitter, tree-sitter-go, rayon, regex, serde, semver), and analysis\ndepends on it. It holds Go *language knowledge* as plain functions, data, and types.\nAnalysis keeps the shim: `GoAnalyzer` (a newtype over `TreeSitterAnalyzer<GoAdapter>`\nthat CANNOT leave), the `GoAdapter` forwarding shell, the SPI block\n(`GoSupport`/edge pass/dead-code bulk), the trait-impl wrappers\n(`GoQueryResolver`/`GoEdgeResolver`/`GoUsageGraphStrategy`), the capability-provider\nimpls, `GoMemoCaches`, and the one-line `impl_program_semantics_provider!` invocation.\nEstimated shim ~1,050 LOC, most of it pre-existing forwarding that merely stays.\n\nMoves to `brokk-bifrost-go` (~10.3k LOC): `packages.rs`, `structural.rs` (after the\nadapter_helpers promotion), the import-analysis logic from `imports.rs`,\n`declarations.rs`, `tests.rs` (Go test detection — production code), `hierarchy.rs`,\n`diagnostics.rs`, the 12 pure `GoAdapter` method bodies as free functions +\n`GO_COGNITIVE_CONFIG`, `GO_CLONE_SYNTAX`, and all six `go_graph` files (the\nwhole-workspace inverted pass reshaped per milestone P0's split). The\n`resources/treesitter/go/*.scm` queries move with the crate; the store epoch salt path\n(`analyzer/store/epoch.rs`, `\"treesitter/go/\"`) must be updated to the new location and\nthe Go `lang_epoch!` salt BUMPED, since query-file relocation changes what the salt\nhashes.\n\nStays in analysis, explicitly, with the reason recorded:\n- `go/semantic.rs` (3,265 LOC): inseparable from the 36.8k-LOC `analyzer/semantic`\n  subsystem; its registration macro textually requires the `TreeSitterAnalyzer` field.\n- `go/artifact.rs` + `go/dependency_discovery.rs` (4,353 LOC): gated on\n  `semantic_model/` (17k LOC) and `process.rs`. Lowering `semantic_model` is a named\n  fleet-phase workstream, not a pilot blocker.\n- `get_definition/go.rs` + `get_type/go.rs` (3,479 LOC): gated on\n  `SemanticModelOverlay`, `ResolutionSession`, `GlobalUsageDefinitionIndex`. Same\n  fleet-phase workstream.\n- `go/cache.rs`: `GoMemoCaches` is shim state (keeps moka out of the go crate and out\n  of core); it may cache go-crate-defined types (e.g. `GoHierarchyIndex`).\n\nGo-crate functions that today take `&dyn IAnalyzer` take `&dyn CodeUnitIndex` (core)\nplus explicit Go side-data instead; every `resolve_analyzer::<GoAnalyzer>` downcast\nhappens in the shim.\n\n## Milestones\n\nP0 — fleet-reusable lowerings into `brokk-bifrost-core` (no Go crate yet; analysis\nre-exports at old paths; every downstream crate compiles unchanged):\n1. `analyzer/usages/model.rs` (755 LOC — imports ONLY CodeUnit/ProjectFile/Range/hash;\n   the highest-leverage single move) and `outcome.rs` (82).\n2. `local_inference.rs` (356), `reference_site.rs` (458; core already has\n   tree-sitter), `receiver_analysis.rs` (624), `type_relations.rs` (121),\n   `graph_core::{ImportEdge, ImportEdgeKind}`, `reexport_seeds.rs` (147).\n3. SPLIT `inverted_edges.rs`: data types (`UsageEdges<K>`, `UsageEdgeWeights<K>`,\n   `CallSite`, `UsageNodeKey`, `NodeKey`, `UsageReferenceCounts`) to core; the driver\n   (`EdgeCollector`, `parse_and_collect`, `build_edge_output`, `ClassRangeIndex`)\n   stays in analysis. `LanguageEdgePass` signatures now traffic in core types.\n4. Extract `UsageScanScope` from `traits.rs` (its co-residents `UsageAnalyzer`/\n   `GraphUsageAnalyzer` name IAnalyzer and stay).\n5. Promote pure helpers: `structural/adapter_helpers.rs` (161),\n   `walk_named_tree_preorder`/`try_walk_...`/`WalkControl`/`collect_parse_errors`/\n   `expanded_comment_start` (~60), `cognitive_complexity::Config` (the plain data\n   struct, not the engine), `canonical_hash.rs` (93). `usages/common` helpers that are\n   pure (`same_node`, `node_text`, `namespace_prefixes`) may ride along;\n   `analyzed_files_for_language` stays (IAnalyzer).\n6. Decide `GO_MODULE_SCOPE_SEGMENT`'s home now (used by symbol_lookup, searchtools):\n   it is Go language knowledge -> it will live in the go crate; for P0 it moves to a\n   spot the crate can re-export from later, or stays put with a note. Also confirm the\n   gate misses `crate::analyzer::`-path imports of Go items (census section 4.3) and\n   extend the gate if cheap.\nAcceptance: workspace gates green; `cargo test -p brokk-bifrost-core --lib` green;\npackage + dependency gates green (code moved between published crates); no downstream\nsource changes.\n\nP1 — the crate and the shim:\n1. Create `crates/bifrost-go`, move the ~10.3k LOC, reshape IAnalyzer params to\n   CodeUnitIndex + side data, wire the shim in `analyzer/go/` (which shrinks to the\n   shim files). Query assets move; epoch salt path updated and salt bumped.\n2. Workspace wiring: root Cargo.toml member + analysis dependency (exact `=` version\n   like core's); `scripts/check-workspace-dependencies.mjs` gains `brokk-bifrost-go`\n   with allowed set `[brokk-bifrost-core]` and analysis's allowed set gains it;\n   `scripts/check-workspace-packages.sh` gains the archive (with the .scm files\n   asserted present); release workflow publish DAG: core -> go -> analysis, with the\n   promotion-evidence gating pattern copied from core's entry;\n   `scripts/release-promotion-workflow.test.mjs` updated (run it);\n   CLAUDE.md release-bootstrap section gains brokk-bifrost-go (crates.io first-publish\n   ceremony). Internal-crate doc stamp on the new crate (registry M2 idiom).\n3. The reach-in gate: `analyzer/go/` remains the per-language exemption dir (now shim\n   only); the gate's LANGUAGE_MODULES etc. need no removal, but verify the gate still\n   passes and that the go crate itself is outside its walk.\nAcceptance: full workspace gates green (fmt, clippy featureless + all-features,\nnextest, doctests); root Go suites named in the census pass; package + dependency +\nrelease-workflow tests green.\n\nP2 — measurement and verdict (the \"works as expected\" test):\n1. Cold `--timings` featureless build (isolated target): analysis frontend expected to\n   shrink ~1.5-2s (10.3k LOC at ~0.17s/kLOC) with the go crate compiling in parallel\n   off the critical path; wall neutral or slightly better. Warm loops per the phase-2\n   methodology. Go-crate unit-test loop measured (expect core-style locality, seconds\n   not tens of seconds).\n2. Reference-differential smoke: identical divergence census on the 11-repo corpus\n   (includes jellydator/ttlcache for Go) between the pre-P0 commit and P1 tip.\n3. Evidence + verdict appended to\n   `.agents/docs/analysis-crate-split-phase2-evaluation-2026-08.md`. PASS =\n   behavior flat + gates green + frontend reduction in the predicted band + shim at or\n   under ~1.3k LOC. On PASS, the fleet proceeds under this plan's sequencing section.\n   On FAIL, stop and report to Jonathan with the numbers.\n\n## Fleet sequencing (on pilot PASS)\n\nPer the seam matrix order, each language repeating the P1 pattern (P0's lowerings are\nalready fleet-shared): Rust, then the remaining MODERATE languages (Python, C#, PHP,\nRuby, C++ — C++ requires generalizing nothing extra per its seam), then the JVM realm\nas ONE crate (Java+Scala+Kotlin+jvm shared realm; prerequisite: lower or generalize\n`ScalaExportInfo` out of `tree_sitter_analyzer.rs`/`store/mod.rs` signatures, and\n`BoundedJavaResolution` out of `receiver_query.rs`), then js_ts last (its four seams\nfrom the matrix). Two shared fleet workstreams scheduled where first needed rather\nthan up front: (w1) lower `semantic_model/` so artifact/dependency-discovery adapters\nand the definition-route files can follow their languages; (w2) the per-language\nsemantic lowerers stay in analysis until/unless `analyzer/semantic` itself is lowered,\nwhich is NOT in stage-3 scope — record the retained mass per language honestly in the\nfinal evaluation. Each fleet crate updates the same wiring set as P1.2 and lands with\nthe same gates; the differential smoke runs at each language whose corpus repo exists,\nelse the language's unit pins are the evidence (Ruby/Kotlin precedent).\n\n## Progress\n\n- [x] P0: core lowerings (4f913fd9, 4a99644e, 3434d692) -- 3,523 LOC to core;\n      inverted_edges split with LanguageEdgePass unchanged; all gates green\n- [x] P1 chunk 1: crate + moves (087a0bac, edd0924b, c520ffdb) -- ~6,030 LOC moved,\n      five census-missed couplings retained ~3.5k in analysis; epoch salt bumped;\n      gates green\n- [x] P1 chunk 2: workspace wiring (87625563) -- dependency graph, ten package\n      archives with .scm assertions, release DAG core -> go -> analysis, workflow\n      policy tests, CONTRIBUTING bootstrap note, stability page\n- [x] P2: measurements + differential (evidence docs committed). Frontend -2.8s\n      (~0.47s/kLOC), go test loop 0.44s steady, wall neutral; behavior byte-identical\n      4/4. Shim ~4.5k vs the ~1.3k bar.\n- [x] Verdict: CONDITIONAL; Jonathan chose option 1 (2026-08-05): fund the three\n      shared workstreams (W1 ParsedFile/ScalaExportInfo lowering, W2 inverted-edge\n      scan contract, W3 CodeUnitIndex enclosing query + bounded-lookup trait), then\n      move Go's residual files (W4), then fleet\n\n## Decision log\n\n- 2026-08-04: Plan created from the seam census (agent-run, verified spot checks; Go\n  seam 21,385 LOC total: ~950 (a)-clean, ~11,850 (b) after ~2,600 LOC of (a)-clean\n  lowerings, ~7,620 (b') gated on semantic subsystems, 909 shim). Pilot excludes the\n  (b') files and the definition routes; they stay behind the shim with named\n  fleet-phase workstreams. Lowered product types go to CORE, not a new mid-crate:\n  every candidate's import list is already core-clean, and core already carries\n  tree-sitter; moka deliberately kept out of core by leaving GoMemoCaches and\n  weighted_cache in analysis.\n- 2026-08-04: inverted_edges.rs split identified as the SPI-touching prerequisite\n  (LanguageEdgePass returns products that must be core types before any language\n  crate can produce them) -- it leads P0.\n- 2026-08-04: Pilot verdict CONDITIONAL. Economics and behavior criteria passed\n  (frontend -2.8s for 6k LOC, 0.44s go test loop, byte-identical differential);\n  the shim-size criterion failed 3.5x because five census-missed couplings\n  (ParsedFile accumulator, DefinitionIndexHandle, enclosing_code_unit,\n  inverted-edge driver, memo shell) retained ~3.5k LOC of Go logic in analysis.\n  Fleet execution halted per the plan's FAIL action; the three shared workstreams\n  that unlock the residue are enumerated in the evaluation doc, with the\n  recommendation to fund them before the fleet rather than repeating the\n  couplings eleven times.\n- 2026-08-04: Epoch-salt rule honored: moving the .scm query files changes the salted\n  path, so the Go lang_epoch! salt bumps in P1 (worktree-agent-pitfalls memory:\n  epoch-salt requirement).\n","numLines":175,"startLine":1,"totalLines":175}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7f7f5682-56a4-438b-b36a-eaa38ba5609e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/jonathan/Projects/bifrost2","sessionId":"8f94fdd6-1d55-4f9d-8f78-532b5b220270","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"bifrost2","slug":"solve-https-github-com-brokkai-bifrost-i-atomic-hopper"}

The\n198-  planned forwarding shim is ~1.05k of it; the other ~3.5k is Go language logic the\n199-  census-missed couplings pinned in place: parse_go_file and its visitors write\n200-  ParsedFile, analysis's private indexing accumulator (617 LOC); diagnostics threads\n201-  DefinitionIndexHandle (1,072); extractor/hits need IAnalyzer::enclosing_code_unit,\n202-  which has no CodeUnitIndex equivalent (650); the inverted-edge driver stayed in\n203-  analysis by P0 design (618); the memo shell is intentional (164).\n204-\n205-Verdict: CONDITIONAL. The pilot proves the economics (better than predicted) and the\n206-mechanism (shims, behavior-flat, gates enforce the boundary), but a fleet run under\n207-today's contracts would retain roughly a quarter of every language in analysis and\n208-repeat these five couplings eleven times. The retained mass is not shim overhead; it\n209-is three shared, fundable workstreams:\n210-\n211-1. Lower the per-file indexing product (ParsedFile and its builders) so language\n212-   declaration walks can leave -- the same workstream that generalizes\n213-   ScalaExportInfo out of tree_sitter_analyzer/store signatures.\n214-2. The analyzer's language-blind model layer.\n//!\n//! Only the four names every module here needs are re-exported at this root;\n//! `brokk-bifrost-analysis` re-exports the full historical surface from its own\n//! `analyzer` module, which is where the analyzer registry, the store, the\n//! usages framework and the language implementations live.\n\npub mod canonical_hash;\npub mod capabilities;\npub mod code_unit_index;\npub mod cognitive_complexity;\npub mod common;\npub mod config;\npub mod definition_lookup;\npub mod dense_id;\npub mod fq_name;\npub mod identifier;\npub mod model;\npub mod parsed_file;\npub mod pool_memo;\npub mod project;\npub mod semantic_diagnostics;\npub mod source_content;\npub mod structural;\npub mod test_paths;\npub mod tree_walk;\npub mod type_relations;\npub mod usages;\n\npub use code_unit_index::{CodeUnitIndex, default_parent_fq_name};\npub use definition_lookup::BoundedDefinitionLookup;\npub use model::{CodeUnit, Language, ProjectFile, Range};\n=== usages/mod ===\n//! The language-blind half of the usages framework.\n//!\n//! These are the products of usage analysis -- hits, references, import and\n//! export bindings, receiver candidates, reference sites -- plus the pure\n//! resolution helpers that operate on them. Nothing here needs an `IAnalyzer`,\n//! a store, or a parsed-file cache, so a language crate can produce these types\n//! without depending on `brokk-bifrost-analysis`.\n//!\n//! The drivers that do need an analyzer handle (the finder, the candidate\n//! providers, the per-language graph strategies, the inverted-edge build's\n//! file fan-out and declaration indexing) stay in `brokk-bifrost-analysis`,\n//! which re-exports every module here at its historical path.\n\npub mod common;\npub mod graph_core;\npub mod inverted_edges;\npub mod local_inference;\npub mod model;\npub mod outcome;\npub mod receiver_analysis;\npub mod reexport_seeds;\npub mod reference_site;\npub mod scan_scope;\n\npub use graph_core::{ImportEdge, ImportEdgeKind};\n=== definition_lookup ===\n//! Bounded lookup of declarations by name.\n//!\n//! [`CodeUnitIndex`] answers from one analyzer's own declarations.  A resolver\n//! or a language scan usually needs a wider view than that -- \"does the\n//! workspace define this fq name at all\" -- and the implementations that can\n//! answer it differ in how much they are allowed to touch: a whole-workspace\n//! index answers from memory, while a persisted analyzer must route the same\n//! question through bounded store queries instead of materializing every\n//! declaration.  [`BoundedDefinitionLookup`] is the contract both satisfy, so a\n//! caller taking `&dyn BoundedDefinitionLookup` cannot accidentally opt into\n//! the unbounded one.\n//!\n//! The trait lives here, below the usages framework that owns its\n//! implementations, so a language crate can ask the question without naming\n//! `brokk-bifrost-analysis`'s resolution model.\n//!\n//! [`CodeUnitIndex`]: crate::analyzer::code_unit_index::CodeUnitIndex\n\nuse crate::analyzer::model::{CodeUnit, Language, ProjectFile};\nuse crate::path_utils::rel_path_string;\n\npub trait BoundedDefinitionLookup {\n    fn fqn(&self, fqn: &str) -> Vec<CodeUnit>;\n    fn fqn_in_language(&self, fqn: &str, language: Language) -> Vec<CodeUnit>;\n\n    /// Exact-fq lookup across *every* language the workspace indexes.\n    ///\n    /// A language-scoped resolver cannot tell \"this fq is not in the workspace\"\n    /// apart from \"this fq belongs to another language's declarations\", so a\n    /// confident cross-workspace boundary claim must consult this before it\n    /// fires (#1174).  The default is the implementation's own scope: bounded\n    /// single-language providers genuinely have no view of other languages, and\n    /// answering same-language keeps them conservative (they can only *fail* to\n    /// suppress a boundary claim, never invent a cross-language hit).\n    fn fqn_in_any_language(&self, fqn: &str) -> Vec<CodeUnit> {\n        self.fqn(fqn)\n    }\n\n    /// Language-blind counterpart of [`Self::package_exists`]; see\n    /// [`Self::fqn_in_any_language`] for why the default is same-language.\n    fn package_exists_in_any_language(&self, package: &str) -> bool {\n        self.package_exists(package)\n    }\n\n    fn file_identifier(&self, file: &ProjectFile, ident: &str) -> Vec<CodeUnit>;\n    fn fqn_direct_children(&self, fqn: &str) -> Vec<CodeUnit>;\n    fn fqn_exists(&self, fqn: &str) -> bool;\n    fn package_exists(&self, package: &str) -> bool;\n    fn package_exists_in_language(&self, package: &str, language: Language) -> bool;\n    fn fqn_prefix_exists(&self, prefix: &str) -> bool;\n\n    fn fqn_candidates(&self, fqns: Vec<String>) -> Vec<CodeUnit> {\n        let mut candidates = fqns\n            .into_iter()\n            .flat_map(|fqn| self.fqn(&fqn))\n            .collect::<Vec<_>>();\n        sort_units(&mut candidates);\n        candidates.dedup();\n        candidates\n    }\n\n    fn file_identifier_in_files(&self, files: &[ProjectFile], ident: &str) -> Vec<CodeUnit> {\n        let mut out = Vec::new();\n        for file in files {\n            out.extend(self.file_identifier(file, ident));\n        }\n        sort_units(&mut out);\n        out.dedup();\n        out\n    }\n}\n\n/// The canonical order definition-lookup results are published in: by source\n/// path, then fq name, then signature, so a `dedup` after it collapses the\n/// same declaration reached through two shards or two spellings.\npub fn sort_units(units: &mut [CodeUnit]) {\n    units.sort_by(|left, right| {\n        rel_path_string(left.source())\n            .cmp(&rel_path_string(right.source()))\n            .then_with(|| left.fq_name().cmp(&right.fq_name()))\n            .then_with(|| left.signature().cmp(&right.signature()))\n    });\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"460df7d4-2fbf-44e9-b28a-a6a5b5298d89","timestamp":"2026-08-05T07:02:31.798Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"//! {"parentUuid":"9224d310-0a78-4618-b38c-ebd42b3a6bf1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d22550a0-292d-4e69-b601-f2ff8a7e9aed","agentId":"a296f2bebb99cef04","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DKAp1kE1k1aZkmvX4hz9Pe","type":"tool_result","content":"6017820b Fix rayon self-deadlock in RustUsageIndex lazy init (#1416 regression)\ncommit 6017820b1b316952cbe70a1e513287d5bce31be4\nAuthor: Jonathan Ellis <jbellis@gmail.com>\nDate:   Sun Aug 2 06:17:15 2026 -0500\n\n    Fix rayon self-deadlock in RustUsageIndex lazy init (#1416 regression)\n    \n    51b3f2b8 parallelized RustUsageIndex::build's per-file phase but left\n    the build running inside OnceLock::get_or_init. When the first\n    usage_index() call arrives on a rayon worker -- reference_differential's\n    pooled group scan does exactly this -- the init's par_iter join steals\n    sibling jobs, and a stolen job that itself calls usage_index()\n    re-enters the initializing OnceLock and deadlocks the pool. Observed\n    live: suite_semantic wedged 90 minutes in\n    rust_same_file_enum_tuple_pattern_round_trips_owner_and_variant_exactly,\n    six workers parked in Once::call and the initializer's own stack\n    containing call_once_force twice (gdb, thread 28, 144 frames).\n    \n    pool_memo.rs's module doc already forbids exactly this: analyzer-level\n    lazy caches whose initializers use rayon must use PoolSafeMemo, which\n    builds serially when reached from inside a pool worker. RustAnalyzer\n    even holds one for the reverse-import index one field above this\n    OnceLock. Switched usage_index to PoolSafeMemo with build(analyzer,\n    parallel) choosing par_iter vs serial iteration, the same shape\n    build_jsts_usage_index already uses.\n    \n    The previously wedged test passes 8/8 repeated runs in 0.15s; the full\n    suite_semantic passes 611/611 in 18.4s. (The extractor.rs hunk is\n    cargo fmt normalization of the merge resolution in the same area.)\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"d323f39f-8d90-49d6-bde3-1bad698c473b","timestamp":"2026-08-05T07:04:37.920Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"6017820b Fix rayon self-deadlock in RustUsageIndex lazy init (#1416 regression)\ncommit 6017820b1b316952cbe70a1e513287d5bce31be4\nAuthor: Jonathan Ellis <jbellis@gmail.com>\nDate:   Sun Aug 2 06:17:15 2026 -0500\n\n    Fix rayon self-deadlock in RustUsageIndex lazy init (#1416 regression)\n    \n    51b3f2b8 parallelized RustUsageIndex::build's per-file phase but left\n    the build running inside OnceLock::get_or_init. When the first\n    usage_index() call arrives on a rayon worker -- reference_differential's\n    pooled group scan does exactly this -- the init's par_iter join steals\n    sibling jobs, and a stolen job that itself calls usage_index()\n    re-enters the initializing OnceLock and deadlocks the pool. Entries are weighed by\n/// their source length; the parsed trees they hold are several times larger, so\n/// keep this comfortably below the process's memory budget. 32 MiB covers every\n/// Rust file of a large workspace (Bifrost's own `src/` is ~20 MiB) in one pass.\nconst RUST_TREE_CACHE_SOURCE_BUDGET_BYTES: u64 = 32 * 1024 * 1024;\n\nstatic RUST_TREES: OnceLock<Cache<Arc<str>, Option<Tree>>> = OnceLock::new();\nstatic RUST_TREE_PARSES: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);\nstatic RUST_TREE_PARSE_REQUESTS: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);\nstatic RUST_TREE_PARSED_BYTES: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);\n\nfn rust_tree_cache() -> &'static Cache<Arc<str>, Option<Tree>> {\n    RUST_TREES.get_or_init(|| {\n        Cache::builder()\n            .max_capacity(RUST_TREE_CACHE_SOURCE_BUDGET_BYTES)\n            .weigher(|key: &Arc<str>, _value: &Option<Tree>| {\n                key.len().min(u32::MAX as usize) as u32\n            })\n            .build()\n    })\n}\n\nfn parse_rust_tree_uncached(source: &str) -> Option<Tree> {\n    RUST_TREE_PARSES.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);\n    RUST_TREE_PARSED_BYTES.fetch_add(source.len(), Ordering::Relaxed);\n    let mut parser = Parser::new();\n    parser\n        .set_language(&tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into())\n        .ok()?;\n    parser.parse(source, None)\n}\n\n/// Parse `source` as Rust, memoized on the exact source bytes.\n///\n/// This is a pure function of its argument, but the Rust usage and definition\n/// resolvers reach it once per *reference site* and once per *candidate\n/// declaration* — `rust_visible_import_resolution`, `lexical_package_at`, and\n/// every `is_rust_*_declaration` shape predicate re-parse the whole enclosing\n/// file each time they are asked a question about it. On a workspace-wide\n/// `scan_usages` that turned into hundreds of thousands of whole-file parses\n/// (issue #1219: ~20 minutes at 1200-1600% CPU on Bifrost's own tree, with\n/// tree-sitter parsing dominating every stack sample).\n///\n/// Memoizing here is observationally identical — the same source bytes always\n/// produce the same tree — and collapses that to one parse per distinct source.\n/// The key is the source text itself, so there is no hash-collision risk and no\n/// invalidation to get wrong: edited content is simply a different key, and the\n/// weighted cache ages stale entries out. `Tree::clone` is a refcount bump\n/// (`ts_tree_copy`), which is also tree-sitter's documented way to hand one\n/// parse to several threads.\npub(crate) fn parse_rust_tree(source: &str) -> Option<Tree> {\n    RUST_TREE_PARSE_REQUESTS.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);\n    let cache = rust_tree_cache();\n    if let Some(cached) = cache.get(source) {\n        return cached;\n    }\n    let parsed = parse_rust_tree_uncached(source);\n    cache.insert(Arc::from(source), parsed.clone());\n    parsed\n}\n\n/// Parse only `[start, end)` of `source` as Rust via included ranges: node\n/// byte/line positions match the original file exactly, and the lexer never\n/// touches the prefix. {"parentUuid":"1bb8bd48-e464-413a-ae8a-bcf4d94d89bb","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d22550a0-292d-4e69-b601-f2ff8a7e9aed","agentId":"a296f2bebb99cef04","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Uja1Uk9g1vQzL1ztS2mMdn","type":"tool_result","content":"//! Whole-workspace inverted edge builder for Rust.\n//!\n//! Walks each file once and resolves every reference to the callee fqn it names,\n//! via the shared [`build_edges`] driver. Rust node fqns are dotted module paths\n//! (`util.format_value`, `service.Service`, `service.Service.new`), so a\n//! reference resolves through the file's import binder plus the path arithmetic\n//! in [`RustAnalyzer::resolve_module_package`]:\n//!\n//! - a `use crate::util::format_value;` binding resolves a bare `format_value`\n//!   to `util.format_value`;\n//! - a `use crate::util;` namespace binding resolves `util::format_value` to\n//!   `util.format_value`;\n//! - a `Type::assoc` path resolves through the type's import / same-file fqn to\n//!   `module.Type.assoc`;\n//! - a same-file item resolves to that declaration's fqn.\n//!\n//! Parameters and `let` bindings shadow same-named imports/items within the\n//! function that introduces them, so a local named like an import does not\n//! produce a false edge (more precise than the forward scan, which shadows\n//! `let`/item names but never parameters). Instance-method dispatch\n//! (`recv.method()`) resolves only when a local receiver fact proves the receiver\n//! type, such as `let recv = Service::new()` or `let recv = make_service()`.\n\nuse super::extractor::{\n    first_generic_type_argument, rust_reference_namespace, type_node_last_segment,\n};\nuse super::hits::{rust_path_is_leading_absolute, rust_path_segments};\nuse crate::analyzer::CodeUnitIndex;\nuse crate::analyzer::rust::lexical_scope::RustLexicalScopeIndex;\nuse crate::analyzer::rust::rust_focused_use_path;\nuse crate::analyzer::rust::{\n    RustBindingSeeds, RustReferenceNamespace, resolve_rust_import_package_scoped,\n};\nuse crate::analyzer::tree_walk::{TreeWalkAction, walk_tree_iterative};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::inverted_edges::{\n    FileEdgeScanInput, PerFileEdges, UsageEdgeBuildOutput, UsageReferenceKind, build_edge_output,\n    classify_reference_node, parse_and_collect,\n};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::receiver_analysis::ReceiverAnalysisOutcome;\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::rust_graph::resolver::{\n    RustBareTokenTreeRole, RustTokenPathRole, RustTokenTreeRoleCache,\n    resolve_rust_token_tree_paths, rust_token_path_segment_is_qualified,\n    rust_unique_nominal_reference_namespace, token_tree_ancestor,\n};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::same_owner::route_same_owner;\nuse crate::analyzer::{\n    CodeUnit, DefinitionIndexHandle, IAnalyzer, ProjectFile, RustAnalyzer, RustReferenceContext,\n    TypeHierarchyProvider,\n};\nuse crate::hash::{HashMap, HashSet};\nuse std::collections::BTreeSet;\nuse std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};\nuse tree_sitter::Node;\n\n/// Build-scoped memo of [`RustAnalyzer::usage_binding_seeds`] results, shared\n/// across the parallel per-file walk. The same candidate symbol is referenced\n/// from many files, and its binding-seed BFS depends only on analyzer state and\n/// the root set: recomputing it per reference is what made whole-workspace\n/// graph construction quadratic (#1504). Dropped when the build returns.\n#[derive(Default)]\nstruct RustSeedsCache {\n    by_roots: Mutex<HashMap<BTreeSet<CodeUnit>, Arc<RustBindingSeeds>>>,\n}\n\nimpl RustSeedsCache {\n    fn seeds(&self, rust: &RustAnalyzer, roots: &BTreeSet<CodeUnit>) -> Arc<RustBindingSeeds> {\n        if let Some(seeds) = self.by_roots.lock().unwrap().get(roots) {\n            return seeds.clone();\n        }\n        // Computed outside the lock: a racing thread may duplicate the BFS for\n        // the same roots, but the first insert wins and the loss is benign.\n        let seeds = Arc::new(rust.usage_binding_seeds(roots));\n        self.by_roots\n            .lock()\n            .unwrap()\n            .entry(roots.clone())\n            .or_insert(seeds)\n            .clone()\n    }\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"76553c46-47c2-4b7b-afba-cca364fac075","timestamp":"2026-08-05T07:05:09.050Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"//! The same candidate symbol is referenced\n/// from many files, and its binding-seed BFS depends only on analyzer state and\n/// the root set: recomputing it per reference is what made whole-workspace\n/// graph construction quadratic (#1504). Dropped when the build returns.\n#[derive(Default)]\nstruct RustSeedsCache {\n    by_roots: Mutex<HashMap<BTreeSet<CodeUnit>, Arc<RustBindingSeeds>>>,\n}\n\nimpl RustSeedsCache {\n    fn seeds(&self, rust: &RustAnalyzer, roots: &BTreeSet<CodeUnit>) -> Arc<RustBindingSeeds> {\n        if let Some(seeds) = self.by_roots.lock().unwrap().get(roots) {\n            return seeds.clone();\n        }\n        // Computed outside the lock: a racing thread may duplicate the BFS for\n        // the same roots, but the first insert wins and the loss is benign.\n        let seeds = Arc::new(rust.usage_binding_seeds(roots));\n        self.by_roots\n            .lock()\n            .unwrap()\n            .entry(roots.clone())\n            .or_insert(seeds)\n            .clone()\n    }\n}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1bb8bd48-e464-413a-ae8a-bcf4d94d89bb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/jonathan/Projects/bifrost2","sessionId":"8f94fdd6-1d55-4f9d-8f78-532b5b220270","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"bifrost2","slug":"solve-https-github-com-brokkai-bifrost-i-atomic-hopper"}

fn parse_file(\n        &self,\n        file: &ProjectFile,\n        source: &str,\n        tree: &Tree,\n    ) -> crate::analyzer::tree_sitter_analyzer::ParsedFile {\n        parse_rust_file(file, Normalized structural search (`query_code`, issue #328).\n//!\n//! Layering, language-independent unless noted:\n//! - [`adapter_helpers`]: small shared mechanics for language adapters.\n//! - `capabilities`: query feature requirements and capability diagnostics.\n//! - [`kinds`]: the normalized node vocabulary with its subtype hierarchy,\n//!   and the role-edge vocabulary.\n//! - [`query`]: the canonical typed query IR and its JSON frontend.\n//! - [`facts`]: the per-file fact arena the matcher runs over.\n//! - [`spec`]: the per-language boundary — kind tables and AST-field role\n//!   extraction (implementations live next to each language's analyzer,\n//!   e.g. `src/analyzer/python/structural.rs`).\n//! - [`extract`]: parse + normalize one file through a spec.\n//! - [`matcher`]: pattern evaluation with captures and containment.\n//! - [`occurrence_rows`]: per-file occurrence rows derived from the arena's\n//!   occurrence roles plus definition resolution (issue #1473).\n//! - [`planner`]: positive-anchor candidate pruning (negation never prunes).\n//! - [`provider`]: the capability trait analyzers expose, plus the\n//!   source-hash-validated facts cache behind it.\n//! - [`search`]: parallel workspace execution and the tool-facing output.\n//!\n//! See `.agents/plans/ISSUE_328_SEARCH_AST_EXECPLAN.md` for the original plan\n//! and `.agents/plans/issue-449-query-code-reference.md` for the public rename.\n\npub(crate) mod adapter_helpers;\npub mod analysis_context;\npub(crate) mod capabilities;\npub(crate) mod execution;\npub mod extract;\npub mod facts;\npub(crate) mod index;\npub mod matcher;\npub mod occurrence_rows;\npub mod planner;\npub mod provider;\npub mod query;\npub mod rune_ir;\npub mod search;\n\n// The normalized kind/role registry and the spec trait a language implements\n// live in `brokk-bifrost-core`, below every grammar; let Some(rust) = resolve_analyzer::<RustAnalyzer>(analyzer) else {\n        return HashSet::default();\n    };\n    let roots = {\n        let _scope = crate::profiling::scope(\"RustQueryResolver::candidate_seeds\");\n        infer_graph_seeds(rust, target).roots\n    };\n    let seeds = {\n        let _scope = seed_result.roots.is_empty() {\n                return GraphUsageOutcome::fallback_safe(\n                    target.fq_name(),\n                    GraphFailureReason::NoGraphSeed(\"no graph seed Go's usage-graph knowledge: the AST vocabulary, the reference resolver, and\n//! the tree-free project/edge indexes both the per-symbol scan and the\n//! whole-workspace inverted pass are built on.\n//!\n//! What is *not* here yet: the two scan bodies, held in\n//! `brokk-bifrost-analysis` until workstream W4 moves them. The inverted\n//! pass's per-file walk is already core-expressible (`scan_go_file` reads a\n//! `FileEdgeScanInput` and returns `PerFileEdges`, both core types); only the\n//! workspace fan-out needs an analyzer handle for each file's declaration\n//! index. The forward scan attributes each hit through\n//! `enclosing_code_unit`, which now lives on `CodeUnitIndex`, so its move is\n//! unblocked too.\n\npub mod ast;\npub mod reference;\npub mod resolver;\n\nuse brokk_bifrost_core::analyzer::CodeUnit;\n\n/// Whether Go's runtime or test harness calls `candidate` without a written\n/// call site.\n///\n/// Lives here beside the other Go usage-graph facts, as C++'s\n/// `is_cpp_global_main` does: dead-code analysis both filters candidates on it\n/// and holds such candidates back from the bulk proof, so it cannot live in\n/// either caller.\npub fn go_implicit_entry_point(candidate: &CodeUnit) -> bool {\n    if !candidate.is_function() {\n        return false;\n    }\n    let name = candidate.identifier();\n    name == \"init\"\n        || name == \"main\" && go_source_declares_package_main(candidate)\n        || candidate\n            .source()\n            .rel_path()\n            .to_string_lossy()\n            .ends_with(\"_test.go\")\n            && go_test_entry_point_name(name)\n}\n\nfn go_source_declares_package_main(candidate: &CodeUnit) -> bool {\n    candidate\n        .source()\n        .read_to_string()\n        .is_ok_and(|source| source.lines().any(|line| line.trim() == \"package main\"))\n}\n\nfn go_test_entry_point_name(name: &str) -> bool {\n    [\"Test\", \"Benchmark\", \"Fuzz\", \"Example\"]\n        .into_iter()\n        .any(|prefix| go_test_name_matches_prefix(name, prefix))\n}\n\nfn go_test_name_matches_prefix(name: &str, prefix: &str) -> bool {\n    let Some(rest) = name.strip_prefix(prefix) else {\n        return false;\n    };\n    rest.chars().next().is_none_or(|ch| !ch.is_lowercase())\n}\n=== analysis go_graph.rs head ===\n//! Go's usage-graph strategy: the analysis-side half.\n//!\n//! The language knowledge -- the AST vocabulary, the reference resolver, the\n//! project and edge indexes -- lives in [`brokk_bifrost_go::graph`]. What stays\n//! here are the scan drivers that need an analyzer handle (`extractor`/`hits`\n//! for `enclosing_code_unit`, `inverted`'s `build_go_edges` for the workspace\n//! fan-out and per-file declaration index) and the trait impls that plug them\n//! into the SPI. `inverted`'s per-file walk itself is pure logic over core\n//! types and the tree-free edge index.\n\nmod extractor;\nmod hits;\nmod inverted;\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::traits::GraphUsageAnalyzer;\n\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::common::{analyzed_files_for_language, language_for_target};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::go_graph::extractor::scan_files_for_target;\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::inverted_edges::{UsageEdgeWeights, UsageEdges};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::model::FuzzyResult;\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::outcome::{GraphFailureReason, GraphUsageOutcome};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::traits::{UsageAnalyzer, UsageQueryResolver, UsageScanScope};\nuse crate::analyzer::{CodeUnit, GoAnalyzer, IAnalyzer, Language, ProjectFile, resolve_analyzer};\nuse crate::hash::HashSet;\npub(in crate::analyzer::usages) use brokk_bifrost_go::graph::reference::{\n    GoReferenceResolution, GoSelectorDescriptor, go_selector_descriptor,\n    go_selector_descriptor_with_scope, resolve_go_reference_with_namespaces,\n};\nuse brokk_bifrost_go::graph::resolver::{\n    GoEdgeIndex, GoGraphSource, GoProjectGraph, TargetSpec, build_go_edge_index, build_go_graph,\n};\nuse std::collections::BTreeSet;\n\npub(crate) use brokk_bifrost_go::graph::go_implicit_entry_point;\npub(crate) use brokk_bifrost_go::graph::resolver::{\n    go_simple_type_name, go_type_name_parts, resolve_go_import_namespaces,\n};\n\n/// Build the whole Go `caller -> callee` edge set in a single inverted pass over\n/// the workspace (see [`inverted`]). Returns `None` when the analyzer exposes no\n/// Go files. `nodes` is the set of node fqns and `keep_file` drops out-of-scope\n=== go_graph/extractor head ===\n//! The per-symbol forward scan: walk each candidate file once looking for one\n//! target's call sites.\n//!\n//! Stays in this crate rather than moving to [`brokk_bifrost_go`] with the rest\n//! of the Go graph: [`ScanCtx`] carries an `&dyn IAnalyzer` because\n//! [`super::hits`] attributes every hit through `enclosing_code_unit`, which is\n//! an analyzer method with no `CodeUnitIndex` equivalent. The Go AST vocabulary\n//! the walk is written in lives in [`brokk_bifrost_go::graph::ast`].\n\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::go_graph::hits::{\n    record_hit, record_self_receiver_hit, record_unproven_hit,\n};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::local_inference::{LocalInferenceConfig, LocalInferenceEngine};\nuse crate::analyzer::usages::model::UsageHit;\nuse crate::analyzer::{IAnalyzer, ProjectFile};\nuse crate::cancellation::CancellationToken;\nuse crate::hash::{HashMap, HashSet};\nuse brokk_bifrost_go::graph::ast::{\n    NON_OWNER_TOKEN, OWNER_TOKEN, SELF_RECEIVER_TOKEN, composite_literal_owner_type_for_key,\n    field_owner_token, for_each_var_spec, is_definition_identifier, is_identifier_node,\n    is_method_receiver_parameter, lhs_identifier_slots, parameter_names,\n    receiver_symbol_from_qualifier, rhs_expressions, selector_parts, type_ref_from_node,\n    var_spec_name_slots, var_spec_names,\n};\nuse brokk_bifrost_go::graph::reference::go_is_top_level_decl;\nuse brokk_bifrost_go::graph::resolver::{\n    GoProjectGraph, ScanBindings, TargetSpec, TypeRef, constructor_call_type_fqns, node_text,\n};\nuse rayon::prelude::*;\nuse std::collections::BTreeSet;","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"8ae66800-90cb-4606-933e-0441c8e219d3","timestamp":"2026-08-05T07:07:09.462Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"//! Returns `None` when the analyzer exposes no\n/// Go files. `nodes` is the set of node fqns and `keep_file` drops out-of-scope\n=== go_graph/extractor head ===\n//! map.iter()\n            .map(|(key, val)| key.len() + val.len() + size_of::<(String, String)>())\n            .sum::<usize>()\n    };\n    let size = map_bytes(&value.named)\n        + match entry {\n                    ExportEntry::Local { local_name } => local_name.len(),\n                    ExportEntry::Default { local_name } => {\n                        local_name.as_ref().map_or(0, String::len)\n                    }\n                    ExportEntry::ReexportedNamed {\n                        module_specifier,\n                        imported_name,\n                    } => module_specifier.len() + imported_name.len(),\n                }\n        })\n        .sum::<usize>();\n    let stars = value\n        .reexport_stars\n module/package name for the file this context resolves from.\n    package: String,\n    /// Dotted module/package name for this file's crate root.\n    crate_package: String,\n    /// local name -> fqn for `use path::Item;` / `use path::func;` named bindings.\n    pub(super) named: HashMap<String, String>,\n    /// local alias -> package for `use crate::util;` namespace bindings.\n    pub(super) namespace: HashMap<String, String>,\n    /// scoped import path -> canonical declaration fqn for namespace imports\n    /// whose members are re-exported from another module.\n    scoped: HashMap<String, String>,\n    /// local name -> canonical declaration fqn for unambiguous glob imports.\n    glob: HashMap<String, String>,\n    /// identifier -> fqn for items declared in this file.\n    pub(super) same_file: HashMap<String, String>,\n}\n\nimpl RustReferenceContext {\n    /// The callee fqn a bare `name` refers to: a named import, a same-file item,\n    /// or a free function imported via `use path::func;` (the binder classifies\n    /// the latter as a namespace whose resolved value is the function's own fqn).\n    pub fn resolve_bare(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str> {\n        self.named","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"399e9c70-2a2e-4e2a-86dd-832e45495a1f","timestamp":"2026-08-05T07:09:26.863Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"use Only the 62-LOC `impl ImportAnalysisProvider for RustAnalyzer` block is shim. |\n| `rust/lexical_scope.rs` | all core (`ImportInfo`, `ImportBinder`/`ImportBinding`/`ImportKind`) **plus `moka`**. |\n| `rust/hierarchy.rs` | all core (`type_relations::{TypeRelation, TypeRelationKind}`, `TypeHierarchyProvider` core `capabilities.rs:197`) except `&RustAnalyzer` (see (c)). |\n| `rust/graph_support.rs` | all core (`ExportEntry`/`ExportIndex`/`ReexportStar` in `core/analyzer/usages/model.rs`) except `&RustAnalyzer` and `PreparedSyntaxTree`. |\n| `rust/diagnostics.rs` | `semantic_diagnostics` and `collect_parse_errors` are core; `SemanticDiagnostic` is core `model.rs:2664`; `DefinitionIndexHandle` → `&dyn BoundedDefinitionLookup` (W3b, Go precedent `a6b7c731`). Residual: `self.rust.reference_context_of` / `.is_type_alias`. |\n| `rust_graph/hits.rs` | `enclosing_code_unit` is now on `CodeUnitIndex` (W3a) — this file's only `IAnalyzer` reason is gone. |\n| `rust_graph/inverted.rs` | already written against the W2 contract (`FileEdgeScanInput`, `PerFileEdges`, `build_edge_output`, `parse_and_collect`) and core `receiver_analysis`/`tree_walk`. Residual: workspace fan-out + `&RustAnalyzer` + `DefinitionIndexHandle`. |\n| `rust/cargo_routes.rs` | core `ProjectFile`/`hash`, `rayon`, `semver`, `toml`. **One non-core import: `tree_sitter_analyzer::PreparedSyntaxTree`** (`cargo_routes.rs:2`, param at `:182`). |\n\n**(c) analysis machinery.**\n\n| construct | files | what it does | does the pilot shim pattern cover it? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `&RustAnalyzer` as the *parameter/receiver type* for language logic | `usage_index.rs`, `graph_support.rs`, `hierarchy.rs`, `imports.rs`, `clones.rs`, `diagnostics.rs`, all four `rust_graph/*`, `get_definition/rust.rs`, `get_type/rust.rs` | **73 distinct methods, 303 call sites.** Roughly half are `CodeUnitIndex`-shaped (`declarations`, `get_analyzed_files`, `parent_of`, `ranges`, `get_definitions`, `signature_metadata`, `enclosing_code_unit`, `get_direct_ancestors/descendants`, `get_members_in_class`, `is_type_alias`, `structural_parent_of`); the other half are Rust-specific memo accessors (`usage_index`, `reference_context_of`, `forward_reference_context_of`, `cargo_routes`, `export_index_of{,_declarations}`, `import_binder_of`, `resolve_module_files/package`, `usage_binding_seeds/names/local_names`, `usage_reference_at`, `files_share_cargo_target`, `candidates_in_*_cargo_*`, `is_rust_*_declaration`, `canonical_rust_hierarchy_type`, `trait_implementer_names`) | **Partly.** `&dyn CodeUnitIndex` + side data works for the first half. The second half is not \"side data you pass in\" — it is a *recursive* memo surface (`usage_binding_seeds` calls back into `usage_index()` which calls `declarations`/`prepared_syntax`). Go had zero analogue. |\n| `impl RustAnalyzer` as the API shape | `mod.rs` 718, `graph_support.rs` 1,210, `usage_index.rs` 642, `hierarchy.rs` 264, `imports.rs` 62 = **2,896 LOC** | Rust language logic written as *inherent methods on the shim type* | **No.** Go's equivalent logic was free functions taking `&GoAnalyzer`; a mechanical `impl` → free-fn rewrite is required before any of this can cross the crate line. |\n| `tree_sitter_analyzer::PreparedSyntaxTree` (crate-private struct, `tree_sitter_analyzer.rs:529`) | `cargo_routes.rs`, `graph_support.rs`, `usage_index.rs`, `semantic.rs`, `rust_graph/extractor.rs`, `rust_graph/resolver.rs` | source + `Tree` + line starts + per-file `ranges`/`children` maps, kept alive together | **No.** Go used it *nowhere* outside `go/semantic.rs`. There is no core equivalent; `ParsedFile` (core) is the indexing product, not the live parse. |\n| `DefinitionIndexHandle` / `GlobalUsageDefinitionIndex` | `diagnostics.rs` (2), `rust_graph/extractor.rs` (7), `rust_graph/inverted.rs` (2), `rust_graph/resolver.rs` (8) | only `fqn()` and `file_identifier()` are actually called | **Yes** — W3b covers it. `resolver.rs:20` already declares the lowered trait `RustDefinitionProvider { fqn, file_identifier }` with impls for both types; it just needs to become `&dyn BoundedDefinitionLookup` and shed its `&RustAnalyzer`/`&dyn IAnalyzer` default-method bodies (`resolver.rs:36-58`). |\n| `resolve_analyzer::<RustAnalyzer>` downcasts | `get_definition/rust.rs` 17, `rust/mod.rs` 6, `rust_graph.rs` 3, `get_type/rust.rs` 2, `get_definition/mod.rs` 1, `rust/diagnostics.rs` 1, `code_quality/dead_code_smells.rs` 1, `bifrost-lsp/.../type_definition.rs` 1 = **32** | | **Yes** — downcast-in-shim. |\n| `analyzer::store::LimitedQueryRows` | `mod.rs`, transitively `get_definition/resolution_session.rs:1` | bounded query rows | **No** — parks with the definition route. |\n| `analyzer::semantic::*` + `semantic::service::ProgramSemanticsLowerer` | `semantic.rs` | IR lowering | **No** — parks (36,794-LOC subsystem, out of stage-3 scope). |\n| `analyzer::semantic_model::*` | `rustdoc_artifact.rs`, `external.rs`, `dependency_discovery.rs` | pack authoring / dependency discovery | **No** — parks on the 16,978-LOC `semantic_model` lowering workstream. |\n| `analyzer::clone_detection`, `analyzer::languages::*`, `weighted_cache` | `clones.rs`, `mod.rs` | | **Yes** — shim by Go precedent. |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Rust-specific hard spots, examined\n\n**`rust/rustdoc_artifact.rs` (2,417).** Yes — same shape as Go's `artifact.rs`. Sole blocker is the 19-name `crate::analyzer::semantic_model::{...}` import at `:12-20`; everything else is core (`canonical_hash`, `hash`, `CancellationToken`) plus `serde` and `rustdoc-types`. **Parks in the same workstream.** One *difference from Go in Rust's favour*: `rust/dependency_discovery.rs` does **not** use `crate::process` — Go's does (`go/dependency_discovery.rs:10`, `run_bounded_process`). Rust reads `RustDependencyApiEvidence` (already core, `crates/bifrost-core/src/analyzer/config.rs:119-146`) and parses JSON. So `external.rs` + `dependency_discovery.rs` + `rustdoc_artifact.rs` = 4,236 LOC gated on `semantic_model` *only*.\n\n**`rust/semantic.rs` (3,236).** Parks exactly like `go/semantic.rs` (3,265): `impl_program_semantics_provider!(RustAnalyzer, RustSemanticLowerer)` at `:25` textually requires the analyzer type, and it consumes `PreparedSyntaxTree` + `crate::analyzer::semantic::*` + `lexical_definitions::formal_parameter_slots_for_owner`.\n\n**`RustUsageIndex` lazy init and the rayon self-deadlock (#1416, `6017820b`).** The contract that must stay shim-side:\n- `RustAnalyzer::usage_index()` (`usage_index.rs:1818`) is a `PoolSafeMemo`, not `OnceLock`, calling `RustUsageIndex::build(self, true)` for the pool-outside path and `build(self, false)` for the pool-inside path. `PoolSafeMemo` lives in core (`crates/bifrost-core/src/analyzer/pool_memo.rs`, 398 LOC), so the *cell* could move — but `build(&RustAnalyzer, parallel)` (`usage_index.rs:1154`) calls `get_analyzed_files`, `declarations`, `prepared_syntax`, and `cargo_routes()` (itself a second `PoolSafeMemo`, `mod.rs:243`). The `parallel` flag threading through `build` is the deadlock fix and must survive any reshaping intact.\n- `RustAnalyzer` holds **seven** lazy cells (`mod.rs:86-110`): four moka `Cache`s (`imported_code_units`, `referencing_files`, `reference_contexts`, `forward_reference_contexts`, `export_indexes`), two `PoolSafeMemo` (`reverse_import_index`, `cargo_routes`, `usage_index`), two `OnceLock` (`package_file_index`, `hierarchy_index`, `type_relations`). Go's `GoMemoCaches` shell was 164 LOC of intentional shim; Rust's is structurally larger and the memo *accessors* are interleaved with logic inside `impl RustAnalyzer` blocks rather than isolated.\n- `warm_usage_analysis` (`usage_index.rs:1829`) itself uses `par_iter` over `reference_context_of` — a third rayon entry point into the same cells.\n- A second, orthogonal global: `lexical_scope.rs:19` `static RUST_TREES: OnceLock<Cache<Arc<str>, Option<Tree>>>` — a 32 MiB moka source-keyed parse memo behind `parse_rust_tree`, reached from **10 files / ~71 call sites** across the seam (41 from `get_definition/rust.rs` alone), with three public test counters on the facade (`rust_tree_parse_count_for_test` etc., re-exported all the way to `/home/jonathan/Projects/bifrost2/src/lib.rs:33`). Moving `lexical_scope.rs` puts **moka into the language crate** — a dependency the pilot deliberately kept out of both core and `brokk-bifrost-go`.\n\n**`get_definition/rust.rs` (7,263; 2.2× Go's `go.rs` at 3,375).** Parks, as expected, but for a *narrower* reason than Go's: it names **no** `semantic_model`/`SemanticModelOverlay` symbol at all. Its gates are (i) `ResolutionSession` (7 sites, `:27/:46/:57/:247/:270`), which imports `analyzer::store::LimitedQueryRows` at `resolution_session.rs:1`; (ii) `AnalyzerRustDefinitionProvider` holding `rust: &'a RustAnalyzer` (`:25-32`); (iii) `use super::*` pulling the 2,349-LOC `get_definition/mod.rs` glob. `get_type/rust.rs` (354) parks with it by construction. Note `get_definition/mod.rs:787` constructs `rust_support` in `DefinitionBatchContext::new` — a per-language field in the shared batch context, so the definition route cannot leave alone even if the store dependency were lowered.\n\n**What the pilot pattern has no answer for.** (1) `PreparedSyntaxTree` in five non-semantic files. (2) `impl RustAnalyzer` as the calling convention for 2,896 LOC of language logic. (3) The `RustDefinitionProvider` trait (`rust_graph/resolver.rs:20-58`) is *almost* the lowered contract Go got for free — but four of its default methods (`forward_reference_context`, `ranges`, `signature_metadata`) take `&RustAnalyzer`/`&dyn IAnalyzer` in their signatures, so the trait itself cannot cross the line unchanged. (4) The moka global parse memo.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Reverse edges\n\nPost-registry, framework files naming Rust items are **15 files, and every one is either a re-export hub, a test, or an allowlisted SPI/capability site.** No stray reach-in.\n\nRe-export hubs (expected):\n- `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/analyzer/mod.rs:197-203` — `pub use rust::{...}`\n- `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/analyzer/usages/mod.rs:87` — `pub use rust_graph::RustExportUsageGraphStrategy`\n- `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/lib.rs:56-63`, `/home/jonathan/Projects/bifrost2/src/lib.rs:25-34` — facade\n- `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/analyzer/languages.rs:727` — registry entry\n- `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/analyzer/multi_analyzer.rs:9,45,261` — `AnalyzerDelegate::Rust`\n- `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/analyzer/usages/get_definition/mod.rs:82,787` — batch-context `rust_support` field\n\nReal production reach-ins (2, both behind a downcast):\n- `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/code_quality/dead_code_smells.rs:952` — `resolve_analyzer::<RustAnalyzer>(...).expect(...)` then `rust.is_rust_public_like_declaration(candidate)` for Rust dead-code scoring. Shim-coverable (one predicate).\n- `crates/bifrost-lsp/src/lsp/handlers/type_definition.rs:97` — `rust.rust_trait_member_implementations(...)` and `rust.is_rust_trait_declaration(...)` for goto-type-definition. **This is the only cross-crate reach-in**, from `bifrost-lsp`; it requires either a capability trait or a re-export through analysis.\n\nOne leak already in core: `crates/bifrost-core/src/analyzer/config.rs:119-146` defines `RustAnalyzerConfig`, `RustDependencyApiEvidence`, `RustSelectedTarget`, `RustPackageApiArtifact` — Rust-named product types living below the language crate. Same pattern as Go's config; not a blocker, but it means the rust crate would depend *up* on core for its own config types.\n\n`cfg(test)`-only uses (verified each sits after the file's last `#[cfg(test)]`): `analyzer/tree_sitter_analyzer.rs:7835` (`use crate::analyzer::rust::RustAdapter`), `analyzer/languages.rs:748,1029`, `analyzer/usages/finder.rs:584,683`, `relevance.rs:2338,2742`, `searchtools/scan_usages.rs:4175-4228`, `searchtools/tests.rs`, `analyzer/structural/search/tests.rs`.\n\nSeparately — **not** in the seam census but real retained Rust knowledge: ~108 `Language::Rust` match arms in shared framework files, concentrated in `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/analyzer/lexical_definitions.rs` (14 arms of Rust grammar: `self_parameter`, `field_pattern`, `closure_expression`, `let_declaration`, …), plus `symbol_lookup.rs:908` (Rust `r#` raw-identifier un-escaping), `usages/receiver_query.rs`, `reference_candidates.rs`, `structural/occurrence_rows.rs`, `analyzer/store/epoch.rs`, and three `bifrost-lsp` handlers. The Go pilot left the equivalent in place; flagging so the final evaluation records it honestly.\n\n---\n\n## 5. crates.io dependencies a `brokk-bifrost-rust` crate would need\n\n| crate | version in `crates/bifrost-analysis/Cargo.toml` | needed by |\n|---|---|---|\n| `brokk-bifrost-core` | path, `=0.8.21` | everything |\n| `tree-sitter` | 0.25.10 | everything |\n| `tree-sitter-rust` | 0.24.0 | `adapter.rs`, `structural.rs`, `lexical_scope.rs`, `cargo_routes.rs`, `declarations.rs`, `mod.rs` (incl. `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY`) |\n| `rayon` | 1.10.0 | `usage_index.rs`, `cargo_routes.rs` |\n| `regex` | 1.12.2 | `tests.rs` |\n| `semver` | 1.0.28 | `cargo_routes.rs` |\n| `toml` | 0.8.23 | `cargo_routes.rs` — **new vs Go**, Go needed neither toml nor semver in-crate |\n| `serde` | — | `dependency_discovery.rs`, `rustdoc_artifact.rs` (only if the artifact files move; both currently park) |\n| `rustdoc-types` | `=0.60.0`, `default-features = false` | `rustdoc_artifact.rs` only — **parked**, so not needed at first landing |\n| `moka` | 0.12.11 `[\"sync\"]` | `lexical_scope.rs`'s `RUST_TREES`. **Only needed if `lexical_scope.rs` moves.** Go's crate has no moka; putting it in the rust crate breaks the pilot's \"moka stays in analysis\" invariant. |\n\n`toml` and `moka` are the two dependency-graph decisions the Go pilot did not have to make.\n\n---\n\n## 6. LOC accounting (Go pilot as calibration)\n\nGo actuals: 21,385 seam → 6,030 moved (28%), ~4,500 analysis residue against a ~1,050 planned shim, ~10,900 parked.\n\n| band | LOC | files |\n|---|---:|---|\n| **Movable now, (a)/(b) clean** | **~7,200** | `declarations.rs` 2,356, `imports.rs` 709 (ex-provider impl), `structural.rs` 501, `tests.rs` 386, `field_roles.rs` 123, `adapter.rs` ~70 (config + free fns), `rust_graph/hits.rs` 638 (W3a unblocked), `rust_graph/inverted.rs` ~1,000 of 1,262 (W2 per-file walk; fan-out stays), `lexical_scope.rs` 1,037 **iff moka goes to the crate** |\n| **Movable after an `impl RustAnalyzer` → free-fn rewrite + `PreparedSyntaxTree` lowering** | **~9,900** | `cargo_routes.rs` 3,483, `usage_index.rs` 3,285, `graph_support.rs` 1,991, `hierarchy.rs` 576, `diagnostics.rs` ~570 of 1,010 (post-W3b, minus the reference-context calls) |\n| **Movable-with-reshaping, usage graph** | **~4,300** | `rust_graph/extractor.rs` 3,737 + `rust_graph/resolver.rs` 1,521 → estimate ~4,300 crosses once `RustDefinitionProvider` sheds `RustAnalyzer`; ~950 stays as scan driver (Go: `go_graph/extractor.rs` 567 + `hits.rs` 83 stayed) |\n| **Parked on `semantic_model`** | **4,236** | `rustdoc_artifact.rs` 2,417, `dependency_discovery.rs` 1,305, `external.rs` 514 |\n| **Parked on `analyzer/semantic`** | **3,236** | `semantic.rs` |\n| **Parked on `ResolutionSession`/store + batch context** | **7,617** | `get_definition/rust.rs` 7,263, `get_type/rust.rs` 354 |\n| **Planned shim** | **~1,470** | `mod.rs` 1,058, `rust_graph.rs` 333, `cache.rs` 49, `clones.rs` 30 + the `LanguageAdapter`/`ImportAnalysisProvider` impl bodies |\n\n**Fleet-run projection under today's contracts** (i.e. if Rust ships the Go P1 pattern with no further lowering): moved ≈ 7,200; analysis residue ≈ 32,150, of which ~15,100 is parked-by-design and ~15,600 is Rust logic pinned by `impl RustAnalyzer`/`PreparedSyntaxTree`. That is a **34% move rate at best vs Go's 28%**, but a shim of ~17k against the plan's ~1.3k bar — an order of magnitude worse than Go's 3.5× miss.\n\n**With the `impl RustAnalyzer` rewrite and a `PreparedSyntaxTree` lowering**, the movable band reaches ≈ 21,400 (54%), residue ≈ 17,900 of which 15,100 is the three named parks — leaving a ~2,800 shim, which is within ~2× of the bar and structurally comparable to what W4 will produce for Go.\n\nFrontend calibration from the pilot (~0.47 s/kLOC): moving 7,200 LOC predicts ≈ **-3.4 s**; moving 21,400 predicts ≈ **-10 s**. Rust is the single largest frontend-reduction opportunity in the fleet.\n\n---\n\n## The three hardest couplings\n\n1. **`impl RustAnalyzer` is the Rust seam's calling convention.** 2,896 LOC of language logic lives as inherent methods on the analyzer type (`mod.rs` 718, `graph_support.rs` 1,210, `usage_index.rs` 642, `hierarchy.rs` 264, `imports.rs` 62), and downstream code reaches it through **73 distinct methods at 303 call sites**. Go had *zero* of this — its logic was already free functions over `&GoAnalyzer`. Roughly half the methods are `CodeUnitIndex`-shaped and the shim pattern covers them; the other half are self-recursive memo accessors (`usage_index` → `declarations`/`prepared_syntax` → `cargo_routes`) that no \"side data in\" signature expresses. This is a prerequisite mechanical rewrite the pilot plan does not contain, and it is the difference between a 34% and a 54% move rate.\n\n2. **`PreparedSyntaxTree` — a crate-private analysis struct on the hot path of five non-semantic Rust files.** `crates/bifrost-analysis/src/analyzer/tree_sitter_analyzer.rs:529`, used by `cargo_routes.rs:2/182`, `graph_support.rs:317/1270/1375`, `usage_index.rs:723/1213/2525`, `rust_graph/extractor.rs:117/1148`, `rust_graph/resolver.rs:1230`. Go touched it only inside `go/semantic.rs`, which parks anyway, so the pilot never had to answer this. Core owns `ParsedFile` (the indexing *product*) but nothing that carries source + live `Tree` + line starts + per-file range/children maps together — and `PreparedSyntaxTree::declaration_node`/`direct_children` read exactly those maps. Either a core `PreparedSyntax`-like contract is added, or ~5,500 LOC of otherwise-clean Rust logic stays behind.\n\n3. **`RustUsageIndex`'s lazy-init contract plus the global moka parse memo.** Two independent, load-bearing pieces of shim-side state. The first is the `PoolSafeMemo` + `build(analyzer, parallel)` shape that `6017820b` installed to fix the #1416 rayon self-deadlock — it must survive any reshaping byte-for-byte in behaviour, and it is entangled with a second `PoolSafeMemo` (`cargo_routes`) and a third rayon entry point (`warm_usage_analysis`'s `par_iter` over `reference_context_of`). The second is `lexical_scope.rs:19`'s `static RUST_TREES` moka cache behind `parse_rust_tree`, reached from 10 files / ~71 sites with three test counters exposed on the public facade (`src/lib.rs:33`); moving it drags **moka into the language crate**, which the pilot explicitly designed against, and leaving it in analysis pins `lexical_scope.rs` (969 prod LOC) plus everything that calls it — including `get_definition/rust.rs`'s 41 sites."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1401,"cache_read_input_tokens":148197,"output_tokens":12736,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1401},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":12736,"cache_read_input_tokens":148197,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1401,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1401,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdjBd7Doxvt2VngNicd62","attributionAgent":"Explore","type":"assistant","uuid":"c195deaf-6521-424a-afe8-8d0ee5a6caa5","timestamp":"2026-08-05T07:13:08.682Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/jonathan/Projects/bifrost2","sessionId":"8f94fdd6-1d55-4f9d-8f78-532b5b220270","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"bifrost2","slug":"solve-https-github-com-brokkai-bifrost-i-atomic-hopper"}
